Search Blog Markets Non Sorted Data Processing Servers Apr 6, 2026 CoreWeave AI Cloud Growth Defies Sector Scrutiny in 2025 According to a report from Yahoo Finance, many artificial intelligence stocks have faced recent pressure as investor focus shifts to profitability. Companies not demonstrating solid returns on AI investments are falling out of favor, with unprofitable firms experiencing significant stock declines. CoreWeave , a cloud computing service provider, is highlighted as a company where eventual profitability is considered nearly certain. The firm is built to serve enterprises seeking to utilize or develop AI solutions without constructing their own data centers. Several major technology companies are listed as customers or partners of CoreWeave, which markets itself as an essential cloud for AI. The company's revenue reportedly increased by 110% year-over-year in the final quarter of 2025. Despite this top-line growth, CoreWeave operated at a loss for that quarter and for the full year of 2025. The losses are attributed to significant spending on technology and infrastructure, which represented a major portion of its annual revenue, as the company positions itself for anticipated future AI data center demand expected to emerge over a multi-year period. Analysts anticipate the company will reach profitability by the end of that five-year timeframe. Interactive table based on the Store Companies dataset for this report. Upgrade All Stocks ETFs Upgrade --> My Profile Pricing Support Sign out Data Center AI CoreWeave Ends 2 GW Texas Data Center Deal With Poolside April 4, 2026 at 8:14 AM \u2022 by MLQ Agent Key Points CoreWeave and Poolside mutually terminated their agreement for Project Horizon, a planned 2-gigawatt AI data center campus in West Texas.[2][4] The partnership, announced in October 2025, included CoreWeave as anchor tenant for the first 250 MW phase with a 15-year lease and option for 500 MW expansion.[1][4] CoreWeave cited its recent $8.5 billion loan facility as enabling a shift to flexible multi-partner leasing from fixed tenant commitments.[3] Poolside now seeks new partners to advance the project after the deal collapsed.[2][3] Advertisement --> CoreWeave and AI startup Poolside have mutually ended their partnership on Project Horizon, a proposed 2-gigawatt data center in West Texas. The companies cited differing strategic directions and timing issues following CoreWeave's securing of an $8.5 billion loan facility.[1][2][3] Partnership Background CoreWeave and Poolside announced their collaboration in October 2025 to develop Project Horizon, Poolside's planned 2 GW AI campus on 568 acres in West Texas.[1][4] Under the initial agreement, CoreWeave was set to provide more than 40,000 NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 GPUs starting in December 2025 and serve as anchor tenant for the first phase of 250 MW capacity under a 15-year lease, with an option for an additional 500 MW.[1][4] The project was structured in eight phases of 250 MW each to support scalable growth for frontier AI training.[4] Reasons for Termination The partnership ended mutually due to diverging strategic paths and timing considerations.[3] CoreWeave's recent $8.5 billion loan facility, backed by a long-term contract with Meta, allowed the company to move away from fixed long-term commitments with single tenants toward a more flexible model involving multiple partners.[3] Poolside, which had planned $2 billion in funding involving Nvidia, now faces a funding gap without a primary tenant.[3] Immediate Next Steps Poolside is actively seeking alternative partners to revive Project Horizon after the collapse of the CoreWeave deal.[2][3] The company emphasized the need for a new anchor tenant to secure financing, as lenders now prioritize projects with long-term contracts from stable companies.[3] Strategic Flexibility Shift CoreWeave's decision reflects a broader shift in the AI infrastructure sector toward capital flexibility. By securing an $8.5 billion loan tied to a Meta contract, CoreWeave gains leverage to avoid high-risk, single-tenant commitments like the 15-year lease for Poolside's 250 MW phase.[3] This move reduces execution risks associated with unproven partners and"

Ark's move into the AI cloud space comes as institutional demand for compute resources continues to grow. The firm did not disclose the average purchase price per share. The buy represents a relatively small position within Ark's overall portfolio. Search Blog Computer, Electronic And Optical Products Electronic Components And Boards Multichip Integrated Circuits: Memories Apr 6, 2026 Nvidia and CoreWeave: AI Investment Dynamics and Performance in 2026 According to a recent report from Yahoo Finance, Nvidia and CoreWeave are both significant entities within the artificial intelligence sector. Nvidia holds a leading position in designing AI accelerators, while CoreWeave operates a cloud infrastructure service built for AI applications. Investment Dynamics and Performance The two companies are not market competitors; Nvidia is a notable investor in CoreWeave. However, both stocks attract capital from those looking to gain exposure to the AI industry. Nvidia's financial performance has been strong, with its revenue increasing by 65% in the fiscal year ending in late January. This follows a 78% rise in the prior fiscal period. Over the past three and a half years, its share price has appreciated by approximately 1,360%. The company's market valuation is near $4.1 trillion, and it maintains substantial liquidity and free cash flow. CoreWeave, which became publicly traded in March of last year, has also seen significant growth. Its stock has risen nearly 85% since its debut, albeit with notable price swings. The company reported a 167% annual increase in revenue, surpassing $5.1 billion for the last calendar year. Its contractual backlog reached $67 billion in the final quarter of that year, and its market capitalization stands at about $39 billion. Market Valuation Considerations Nvidia currently trades at a price-to-earnings multiple of 35, which is above the average for the S&P 500 . Given its revenue expansion, some analysts view this valuation as reasonable. The scale of Nvidia's existing market cap, however, presents a challenge for future exponential stock price growth. CoreWeave's smaller market size may offer a different growth profile for investors, supported by its rapidly expanding backlog and revenue.     "title": "CoreWeave's new debt facility: beyond lower interest rate and $8.5 billion capacity - Deep Quarry",
    "link": "https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMieEFVX3lxTE4yemc2ekEzWUJ2V21aWWdjOGdRb196NWR4RWxGNDFqejdndzhwRTFqOUdmUVNEeU5mMkQyUUJTWEliWkJYSDRaUE1zYmlxVDhpRUNlbjlERkEtM3luZEJMTzFCTk5NS3FqeDM2QU54MXlWUG5oRWNTVA?oc=5",
    "published": "2026-04-04T21:17:48Z",
    "content": "CoreWeave's new debt facility: beyond lower interest rate and $8.5 billion capacity Deep Quarry Subscribe Sign in CoreWeave's new debt facility: beyond lower interest rate and $8.5 billion capacity The new structure ties borrowing to execution, not just hardware. Olga Usvyatsky Apr 04, 2026 \u2219 Paid 8 2 Share On March 30, 2026, CoreWeave (Ticker: CRWV) closed an $8.5 billion Delayed Draw Term Loan facility (DDTL 4.0) \u2014 a significant expansion of its prior GPU-backed financing structures. At first glance, the new facility appears more favorable, with lower pricing (SOFR + 2.25% in DDTL 4.0 vs. SOFR + 4.00% in DDTL 3.0) and larger upfront capacity. But those headline improvements obscure a more consequential shift: the structure of the facility has evolved in ways that change how leverage is determined, monitored, and constrained. The differences are not just incremental \u2014 they reflect a move away from a more formulaic, asset-based framework toward a more complex, project-style financing model. Francine McKenna and I discussed the terms and conditions of CoreWeave\u2019s DDTL 3.0 facility in our previous post. CoreWeave walks a debt tightrope, counting on key customers to be its safety net Olga Usvyatsky and Francine McKenna \u00b7 Jan 27 Read full story Below is a high-level overview of the key differences (with a deeper dive behind the paywall). At a high level, the differences between DDTL 3.0 and DDTL 4.0 include: Lower interest rate, higher borrowing capacity, and looser covenants. Pricing improves materially and capacity increases, but not simply as a more generous version of the same structure. Shift from asset-based limits to execution-based borrowing. Borrowing is no longer capped by depreciated GPU value and instead depends on operational milestones. Power access is now embedded in the structure of the facility. Power availability and power cost volatility are explicitly addressed through funding terms conditions. Figure 1 \u2013 Comparison of Selected Terms of CoreWeave\u2019s DDTL 3.0 and 4.0 Facilities Source: CoreWeave\u2019s DDTL 3.0 and DDTL 4.0 facilities. I discuss the shift in borrowing terms in more details behind the paywall. Please consider subscribing! Deep Quarry is a reader-supported publication. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 5, 2026 Coatue Management Exits CoreWeave Stake Ahead of Stock Decline Philippe Laffont's hedge fund, Coatue Management , has sold its entire stake in CoreWeave. The fund's quarterly regulatory filing indicates this position was fully disposed of in the fourth quarter. Coatue had held a stake in CoreWeave since 2024, leading a funding round that year. The position grew following the company's public debut in early 2025 and became the fund's largest holding by the end of the second quarter. The fund began reducing its stake in the third quarter before the complete sale. The timing of the sale preceded a significant decline in CoreWeave's share price, which fell approximately half of its value starting in October. CoreWeave operates in the AI data center sector and utilizes long-term customer contracts to secure financing for expansion, a strategy that results in high leverage relative to its earnings. Investor sentiment shifted after the company reported a delay last fall involving a contractor responsible for building out data center capacity. This event contributed to the stock's decline. Despite this setback, demand for computational resources remains strong, and CoreWeave's primary customers have maintained their relationships with the company. 1. Nvidia and Meta Both Bet on Nebius \u2013 Up 400% in a Year, Is It the Next CoreWeave? Published on: Apr 5, 2026 Author: Sunlight Xiang Share article: Two tech giants are backing the same dark horse in AI cloud infrastructure. Meta signed a $27 billion deal. Nvidia invested $2 billion. And Nebius Group (NBIS) \u2013 up nearly 400% over the past year \u2013 is leaving rival CoreWeave (up 109%) in the dust. With a $25.2 billion market cap, Nebius is still smaller than CoreWeave\u2019s $40.7 billion. But the gap is closing fast. Dual Heavyweight Backing In mid-March, Nebius landed a five-year AI infrastructure agreement with Meta worth up to $27 billion \u2013 $12 billion for dedicated capacity plus a $15 billion option for additional purchases. Around the same time, Nvidia announced a $2 billion strategic stake, helping Nebius deploy over 5 gigawatts of Nvidia systems through 2030. Notably, Nvidia already owned more than 12% of CoreWeave as of late 2025. Betting on both horses says everything about how hot this race is. Scale vs. Speed CoreWeave is still the larger player: $40.7 billion market cap, 43 data centers, 850 megawatts of live power. Nebius had just 170 MW at the end of 2025 but plans to hit 800 MW to 1 GW by end of 2026. But growth tells a different story. Nebius stock: +400% in 12 months CoreWeave stock: +109% in the same period Revenue in Q4: Nebius surged 547% year-over-year to $228 million. CoreWeave grew 110% to $1.57 billion. Both Burning Cash to Grow Neither is profitable yet. Both are spending heavily on GPUs and data centers. Nebius (Q4) CoreWeave (Q4) Net loss $173M $284M Quarterly capex $2B+ $8.2B CoreWeave expects 2026 revenue of $12-13 billion, backed by over $46 billion in contracts with OpenAI and Meta. Nebius holds major deals with Meta ($27B) and Microsoft (up to $19.4B). Risks and the Bigger Picture AI compute rental prices are up 40% since October \u2013 demand remains strong. Nebius recently raised $4 billion in convertible notes and is building a 300 MW data center in Finland, targeting 3 GW of contracted business by 2026. But high capex and fierce competition mean volatility is guaranteed. Nebius offers faster growth, elite customers, and Nvidia\u2019s blessing. CoreWeave still leads in scale and execution. For investors, Nebius is a high-upside bet: if it converts its backlog into profits, the sky\u2019s the limit. If not,"

Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 4, 2026 CoreWeave's 2025 Revenue Hits $5.1B Amid $21B Debt and Customer Risks According to a report from Yahoo Finance , CoreWeave, which trades on the NASDAQ , has experienced significant financial expansion. The company's revenue for 2025 reached $5.1 billion, representing a 168 percent increase from the prior year. Management has forecast that revenue will more than double in 2026. Furthermore, the firm's backlog of future business grew by over 300 percent to a total of $66.8 billion. Financial Leverage and Interest Expenses This growth is accompanied by substantial financial obligations. CoreWeave's total debt exceeds $21 billion, a notable increase from under $8 billion in 2024. A large portion of this debt carries a variable interest rate, averaging approximately 11 percent. In the final quarter of 2025, the company's interest expense was $388 million. This quarterly cost means nearly one-third of its revenue is allocated to interest payments. Customer Concentration and Associated Risks The business relies heavily on a small group of major technology and artificial intelligence firms for its revenue. Microsoft and other large-scale cloud providers constitute the majority of its customer base. These entities are considered natural competitors, which could lead them to internalize computing needs in the future. Another key client, OpenAI, is projected to incur substantial losses, including a $14 billion loss for 2026 and potential cumulative losses through 2029. To meet its commitments, OpenAI must secure ongoing large-scale funding, though recent capital raises include significant contingencies and conditions. Potential Economic Headwinds The coming year presents specific challenges. Economic conditions may slow, and geopolitical tensions could elevate the risk of a recession. Concurrently, a potential resurgence in inflation might prompt the Federal Reserve to increase interest rates. Such a move would directly impact CoreWeave's variable-rate debt, potentially raising its borrowing costs further and creating obstacles in securing new financing necessary to convert its backlog into revenue. 1.     "published": "2026-04-02T00:57:05Z",
    "content": "CoreWeave's $8.5 Billion Financing & 12% Stock Surge: A Deep Dive Search Newsletters Editions Home Latest Business News Money & Markets Tech & Innovation A.I. Lifestyle Leadership \u2709\ufe0f Emails \ud83c\udfa7 Podcasts Untitled CoreWeave's $8.5 Billion Financing & 12% Stock Surge: A Deep Dive CRWV secured an $8.5B GPU-backed loan with investment-grade ratings, cutting borrowing costs and supporting AI infrastructure expansion despite rising debt risk Originally published on Zacks Investment Research Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Add Quartz on Google CoreWeave, Inc . CRWV recently closed an $8.5 billion delayed draw term loan facility (DDTL 4.0), highlighting how financial markets are evolving to support the explosive growth of AI. With the DDTL 4.0 Facility, CoreWeave can access capital in stages as it scales its infrastructure. Initially, the company can borrow up to $7.5 billion, with the flexibility to expand to $8.5 billion as its underlying assets mature and stabilize. This is the first time in history that a loan backed by HPC assets, such as GPUs, has received investment-grade ratings (A3 by Moody\u2019s). The first investment-grade GPU-backed financing confirms strong AI infrastructure demand, reduces capital costs, increases profitability potential and caused a 12% stock jump on the last trading day as investors bet on AI infrastructure\u2019s long-term value. Investment-grade ratings lower CRWV\u2019s borrowing costs and boost investor confidence, enabling it to secure favorable financing rates (SOFR + 2.25% floating, roughly 5.9% fixed) despite operating in a capital-intensive, emerging technology sector. Related Content OpenAI reshuffles leadership as 2 executives take medical leave JPMorgan Chase CEO Jamie Dimon warns of geopolitics and AI risks in his annual letter However, CoreWeave faces risks, including high debt from its aggressive, debt-funded expansion that depends on continuous revenue growth, customer concentration among a few large AI and hyperscale clients, reliance on NVIDIA for GPU supply and pricing power, and execution risks in scaling data centers, such as power constraints, construction delays and operational complexity. Over the past 12 months, it has secured approximately $28 billion in combined equity and debt financing. As of Dec. 31, 2025, long-term debt was $14.7 million compared with $5.5 million a year earlier. It expects 2026 CapEx of $30\u2013$35 billion, more than doubling 2025 levels, which will likely pressure near-term profits due to the timing gap between upfront costs and the gradual revenues from new capacity. Financing Playbook of CRWV\u2019s Market Competitors Nebius Group N.V. NBIS is exploring corporate debt and asset-backed financing, with its unused ATM equity program and stakes in ClickHouse and Avride as additional future capital sources. NBIS operates in a dynamic environment driven by macroeconomic uncertainty, rising operating expenses and significant capital requirements. It plans to invest $16-$20 billion in capex in 2026 to expand its AI infrastructure. While this supports long-term growth, such elevated spending could increase financial risk if revenue growth does not keep pace with the company\u2019s capital-intensive expansion"

CoreWeave (CRWV) Stock After the $8.5 bil GPU Loan: Rerating or Leverage? Author: Charon N. Published on: 2026-04-06 --> Key Takeaways The new financing strengthens the bull case around CoreWeave stock. Lower funding costs improve the quality of the growth story. Heavy debt and capital intensity still limit financial flexibility. Strong demand visibility does not remove customer concentration risk. CoreWeave stock has a fresh catalyst, and it goes deeper than another AI headline. The company\u2019s new $8.5 billion GPU-backed loan has revived the rerating debate because it suggests Wall Street is beginning to treat contracted AI infrastructure as financeable collateral rather than speculative growth. That is why the latest move matters. On March 31, CoreWeave closed a non-recourse delayed draw term loan facility with investment-grade ratings of A3 from Moody\u2019s and A (low) from DBRS. Shares rose sharply on the news, but the bigger question is whether cheaper capital changes the stock\u2019s long-term profile or simply adds another layer to an already leveraged expansion model. Why The $8.5 Billion GPU Loan Matters For CoreWeave Stock The new facility is important because it is not a routine corporate borrowing. CoreWeave said it is the first investment-grade rated financing secured by high performance computing infrastructure and an associated customer contract. The structure allows an initial borrowing capacity of about $7.5 billion, rising to $8.5 billion as assets stabilize. The terms also matter. The facility includes a floating tranche at SOFR + 2.25% and a fixed tranche at about 5.9%, with maturity in March 2032. That"

Circle Trades at a Higher P/B: Should You Buy, Sell or Hold the Stock? 4 min read CRCL Shares of Circle Internet Group CRCL are currently overvalued, as suggested by its Value Score of F, indicating that the market is pricing in very high growth expectations for the company. In terms of the 12-month price/book ratio, CRCL is trading at 6.69X, significantly higher than the Zacks Financial Miscellaneous Services industry\u2019s 2.5X and the Zacks Finance sector\u2019s 4.09X. CRCL shares are also trading at a higher multiple than those of its peers, including Futu Holdings FUTU, IREN Limited IREN and Strategy Inc MSTR. Futu Holdings, IREN Limited and Strategy have current P/B ratios of 3.77X, 4.6X and 0.91X, respectively. Price/Book Ratio -TTM At such elevated valuation levels, the key question is whether Circle can justify the premium through its fundamentals and future growth potential. Let\u2019s dig deeper to find out. CRCL Broadens Reach Across Digital Assets Circle\u2019s expansion into a multi-asset digital ecosystem is emerging as a key driver of its long-term growth, as the company moves beyond a single stablecoin model to a broader suite of onchain financial instruments. While USDC remains the core foundation, Circle has expanded into additional digital assets such as EURC (a euro-denominated stablecoin) and USYC (a tokenized money market fund), enabling it to address a wider range of financial use cases. EURC has demonstrated strong momentum, with circulation growing significantly year over year, while USYC provides a yield-bearing alternative that complements payment stablecoins by supporting collateral and treasury workflows. This multi-asset approach allows Circle to participate not only in payments but also in trading, liquidity management and institutional finance, expanding its total addressable market. The company\u2019s strategy further strengthens this ecosystem through interoperability and liquidity services, such as Circle Mint and xReserve, which facilitate minting, redemption, custody and cross-chain compatibility of its digital assets. By enabling seamless movement and interaction across assets and blockchains, Circle reduces fragmentation and improves user experience, reinforcing network effects. As adoption grows across enterprises, financial institutions and developers, this multi-asset ecosystem is expected to deepen engagement, increase transaction volumes and ultimately drive sustainable long-term growth for CRCL. CRCL\u2019s Growth Backed by Infrastructure Evolution Circle\u2019s future growth relies on its expanding infrastructure. The company is developing its own Layer-1 blockchain, Arc, along with tools for developers and interoperability. Arc aims to serve as a financial system for the internet, enabling businesses to move real-world transactions on-chain with fast processing, stablecoin-based fees and privacy features. It is already gaining early adoption, with over 100 participants testing the platform before its planned mainnet launch. Circle is investing heavily in developer infrastructure alongside its core blockchain. Its programmable wallets and smart contract platforms make it easier to create and deploy"

Jason Andrew Mar 24, 2026 12 min read Share 0 Image: SmartCompany via IREN and Canva In 2013, two Aussie brothers bought some Bitcoin, only to sell, thinking the market had \u2018topped\u2019. \u201cYeah nah, mate, that\u2019s probably as high as she goes.\u201d But five years later, they took another look at Bitcoin, betting that Satoshi\u2019s invention was the future of digital currency by starting a crypto mining project in Canada. It went public in 2021, only to have its stock crushed by 95% in the 2022 Bitcoin crash. Smarter business news. Straight to your inbox. For startup founders, small businesses and leaders. Despite almost losing the company to debtholders, the company survived and in 2023, pivoted the business to go all in on the AI thesis. Today, IREN is a US$12 billion (AU$17.15 billion) company and would be the 37th largest company on the ASX if not for a US listing. Up over 300% this year, IREN is ripping on the AI thesis. But are we in a bubble? Is the AI hype real? This is the story of how two Aussie brothers built a $10 billion empire with IREN, and a breakdown of the state of the data centre market. Image: SBO Financial The IREN story IREN started in 2018 when brothers Daniel and Will Roberts saw an opportunity to use renewable energy to mine Bitcoin at attractive returns. In the words of the IREN prospectus: \u201cWe are a Bitcoin mining company. We build, own and operate data centres and electrical infrastructure for the mining of Bitcoin primarily powered by renewable energy\u201d. IRENs , Strategic IREN's Strategic Pivot Faces Scrutiny Amid Volatile Market Reaction 07.04.2026 - 05:04:52 | boerse-global.de IREN shares tumble as its shift from Bitcoin mining to AI cloud services faces execution risks, a wider-than-expected loss, and plans for a $6B capital raise. IREN's Strategic Pivot Faces Scrutiny Amid Volatile Market Reaction - Foto: \u00fcber boerse-global.de The shares of IREN have experienced significant volatility, declining approximately 20% as the market grapples with the company's ambitious transformation. Its strategic shift from a Bitcoin mining operation to a provider of artificial intelligence cloud services is proving to be a capital-intensive endeavor, with recent quarterly results and substantial funding requirements weighing on investor sentiment. While management is committing heavily to new Nvidia hardware, doubts are emerging on Wall Street regarding the pace and execution of this transition. Analyst Sentiment Reveals Deep Divisions The investment community is sharply divided on IREN's prospects. Freedom Capital Markets recently initiated coverage with a \"Hold\" rating and a $36 price target, cautioning about significant execution risks and potential delays in transitioning from specialized cryptocurrency computing hardware to GPU-based infrastructure. In stark contrast, Bernstein maintains a bullish \"Outperform\" rating with a $125 price target. Analysts there commend the disciplined build-out of the AI cloud platform and highlight the company's substantial, uncontracted energy portfolio of 4.5 gigawatts as a critical competitive advantage. This power capacity is seen as a major lever, particularly as large hyperscale clients like Microsoft seek reliable energy sources for their AI operations. Should investors sell immediately? Or is it worth buying IREN? Quarterly Results and Capital Plans Disappoint The recent sell-off was triggered by a combination of fundamental underperformance and external headwinds. For its second fiscal quarter, IREN's results fell short of expectations. The company reported a loss per share of $0.52, significantly wider than the forecasted loss of $0.11. Revenue, coming in at just under $185 million, also missed analyst estimates. Further unsettling investors were new U.S. tariffs on AI chips and the announcement of a potential capital raise of up to $6 billion. Co-CEO Daniel Roberts defended the fundraising plan as a purely optional strategic move to capitalize on the AI growth wave. However, following the weak quarterly figures, the market remains deeply concerned about potential shareholder dilution. This breakdown is designed to help you frame that question clearly. The stock has recently posted returns of 22.0% over 7 days, 26.0% over 30 days, 25.1% year to date, and 434.6% over the past year, which naturally raises questions about risk, sustainability, and what is already reflected in the price. Recent coverage has focused on Nebius Group as a fast moving name in the software space, with attention on how its business model and market positioning relate to its sharp share price moves. Headlines have also highlighted growing investor interest and trading activity, which can both support momentum and increase volatility. Nebius Group currently scores 3 out of 6 on Simply Wall St's valuation checks, giving it a valuation score of 3 . Advertisement Approach 1: Nebius Group Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) Analysis A Discounted Cash Flow, or DCF, model estimates what a business could be worth today by projecting its future cash flows and then discounting those back to a present value. For Nebius Group, the model used is a 2 Stage Free Cash Flow to Equity approach. The latest twelve month free cash flow is a loss of about $3,545.57 million. Analysts provide free cash flow estimates through 2030, with projections in this period moving from losses into a forecast of $4,780 million of free cash flow in 2030. Beyond the analyst horizon, Simply Wall St extends the cash flow path using its own assumptions for several more years. Aggregating and discounting these projected cash flows gives an estimated intrinsic value of about $606.89 per share. Compared with the recent share price around $112.54, the DCF output points to an implied discount of roughly 81.5%. This output suggests Nebius Group screens as significantly undervalued on this model alone. Result: UNDERVALUED Our Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) analysis suggests Nebius Group is undervalued by 81.5%.
